=== AGENTS.md
# Infinity Games Interview Workspace

This repository is the workspace for a timed, AI-assisted browser game prototype
(the Infinity Games "Vibe Coder" assessment, 4-hour window). Every agent working
here (Claude Code, Antigravity, Codex) follows these rules. These rule and skill
files are part of the submitted prompt log.

Before working, read `context/preferences.md`, `context/brief.md` (once a brief
exists), `PLAN.md` and `TASKS.md`. Check `processes/` for an existing workflow and
`knowledge/` for prior lessons before inventing a new approach.

## Ground rules of the assessment

- **No game code exists before the window opens.** Anything related to the
  mechanic, the prototype or the game is created inside the window, with AI, and
  appears in the prompt log. This repo starts with a blank page, rules, skills
  and game-independent tooling only.
- **It is opened in a desktop browser.** Mouse and keyboard first. Touch is a bonus
  only when it comes free with pointer events; no cross-device work.
- **The prototype is a collaboration between the owner and AI.** The owner may edit

1	=== AGENTS.md
2	# Infinity Games Interview Workspace
3	
4	This repository is the workspace for a timed, AI-assisted browser game prototype
5	(the Infinity Games "Vibe Coder" assessment, 4-hour window). Every agent working
6	here (Claude Code, Antigravity, Codex) follows these rules. These rule and skill
7	files are part of the submitted prompt log.
8	
9	Before working, read `context/preferences.md`, `context/brief.md` (once a brief
10	exists), `PLAN.md` and `TASKS.md`. Check `processes/` for an existing workflow and
11	`knowledge/` for prior lessons before inventing a new approach.
12	
13	## Ground rules of the assessment
14	
15	- **No game code exists before the window opens.** Anything related to the
16	  mechanic, the prototype or the game is created inside the window, with AI, and
17	  appears in the prompt log. This repo starts with a blank page, rules, skills
18	  and game-independent tooling only.
19	- **It is opened in a desktop browser.** Mouse and keyboard first. Touch is a bonus
20	  only when it comes free with pointer events; no cross-device work.
21	- **The prototype is a collaboration between the owner and AI.** The owner may edit
22	  designs and make changes by hand; say so honestly in the written answers.
23	- **Honesty over polish.** The prompt log is complete and unedited. If something
24	  breaks, ship what works and say what went wrong.
25	- **The owner's raw notes are sacred.** Any raw thoughts the owner writes or pastes
26	  (playtests, ideas, reactions) are saved verbatim — typos included — as a new file in
27	  `authors-raw-notes/`, and never edited afterwards by anyone. Analysis, summaries or
28	  corrections go in a separate file (e.g. `knowledge/`) that may quote them. The folder
29	  is part of the submission under "Author's raw notes".
30	
31	## Roles
32	
33	- **Claude Code is the admin.** It owns the brief, the design, every UI/visual
34	  file, the plan and all decisions, and it reviews everything a worker produces.
35	- **Gemini (Antigravity IDE, 3.8 Flash High) is the worker.** It takes bounded,
36	  non-visual tasks through the mailbox `claude antigravity and others/`: tests,
37	  data files, research, asset conversion, log export, first drafts of written
38	  answers. Claude writes `tasks/T<n>.md`; the owner tells the worker "claude has
39	  work for you"; the worker writes `reports/T<n>.md` and touches only the files
40	  its task lists. Any other agent used as a worker (Antigravity CLI, Freebuff)
41	  follows the same folder's README.
42	- Workers never edit `design/`, stylesheets, `public/index.html` or rendering code
43	  unless their task explicitly names them.
44	- The owner makes design and scope decisions. Agents look up facts themselves.
45	
46	## Priorities
47	
48	1. **Looks first; mechanics only need to be good enough.** A small, beautiful,
49	   coherent prototype of the core loop beats a complete but ugly one. When time is
50	   short, cut mechanics, never polish. Optional work is ranked by what the
51	   assessor sees (video, first two minutes of play) and each item has a time gate
52	   in `PLAN.md`; unseen work (server telemetry, backends, accounts, consent
53	   banners) is not built in the window — it becomes the "next 4 hours" answer.
54	2. **Design first, build backwards.** The chosen Figma frames are the spec. Build
55	   each screen to match its frame and compare against it before calling it done.
56	   Never improvise UI that the design does not show.
57	   **Design gate (absolute):** no UI code — `public/index.html`, CSS, view,
58	   screens, controller, rendering, HUD — before the owner has picked a direction
59	   and Claude has locked it (`**Design locked:**` filled in `PLAN.md`,
60	   `design/tokens.css`, a reference PNG in `design/frames/`). Until then only
61	   logic is built: engine, config, RNG, storage, audio, tests. If the owner asks
62	   to "get a game first", remind them of this rule and offer the fast path
63	   (two directions in ~10 minutes) instead; never work around it. A Claude Code
64	   hook (`tools/design-gate.mjs`) blocks UI writes and `npm run verify` refuses
65	   to deploy until the design is locked.
66	3. **Something live at all times.** Deploy as soon as the first playable exists,
67	   then after every milestone. Never break the last good deploy.
68	4. **The brief wins.** Start screen, levels, scores and fanfare are defaults, not
69	   mandates. Drop any default the brief contradicts or that the core loop doesn't need.
70	5. **Honest evidence.** Keep implemented, agent-verified and owner-accepted apart.
71	   Never call unverified work done.
72	
73	## Working rules
74	
75	- Build the game with the `game-scaffold` skill: pure game logic in
76	  `public/src/engine.js` (no DOM, timers or unseeded randomness) so `npm test` can
77	  verify it headless in Node; tuning numbers in one `CONFIG` object.
78	- Before any deploy: `npm test` and `npm run smoke` must both pass.
79	- Desktop stage: the game sits in a centred stage on a desktop screen (portrait
80	  phone-shaped by default for a mobile game), driven by mouse; Space/Enter start
81	  or restart, Esc pauses. Use pointer events so touch works for free.
82	- Audio unlocks on the first click or key press. Sound comes from the `game-audio`
83	  skill (ElevenLabs or procedural); never ship silence or harsh beeps.
84	- Celebration effects fire only on real events (a clear, a win, a best score).
85	  No ambient sparkle, glow or particles that don't mean anything.
86	- Prefer graphics over text in the game UI. Keep text short where it is needed.
87	- Skills live in `.agents/skills/` (the source). After editing one, run
88	  `npm run skills:sync`; `.claude/skills/` holds synced copies, never symlinks.
89	- Keep `.env`, keys, tokens and account IDs out of Git. `.env.example` holds names
90	  with empty values only.

// Poly Pizza (low-poly 3D models, CC0 / CC-BY) from the command line. Game-independent.
//   npm run models -- search "police car" [--cc0] [--category 3] [--animated] [--limit 10]
//   npm run models -- get <id>                       (metadata only)
//   npm run models -- download <id> [name]           (inside the window only)
// Key: POLY_PIZZA_API_KEY in .env (x-auth-token header, also needed on static.poly.pizza).
// Downloads go to public/assets/models/<name>.glb and the attribution is appended to
// public/assets/models/CREDITS.md, so the browser never sees the key.
// API spec: https://poly.pizza/apispec/v1.1.yaml (base https://api.poly.pizza/v1.1,
// 100 requests per second). Categories: 0 Food & Drink, 1 Clutter, 2 Weapons,
// 3 Transport, 4 Furniture & Decor, 5 Objects, 6 Nature, 7 Animals, 8 Buildings,
// 9 People & Characters, 10 Scenes & Levels, 11 Other.
import { readFile, writeFile, appendFile, mkdir } from 'node:fs/promises';
import path from 'node:path';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';

const root = path.resolve(path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), '..');
const API = 'https://api.poly.pizza/v1.1';
const [cmd, ...rest] = process.argv.slice(2);
const flag = (name) => rest.includes(`--${name}`);
const opt = (name) => { const i = rest.indexOf(`--${name}`); return i >= 0 ? rest[i + 1] : undefined; };
const args = rest.filter((a, i) => !a.startsWith('--') && !(i > 0 && ['--category', '--limit'].includes(rest[i - 1])));

const key = process.env.POLY_PIZZA_API_KEY || (await readFile(path.join(root, '.env'), 'utf8').catch(() => ''))
  .match(/^POLY_PIZZA_API_KEY=(.+)$/m)?.[1]?.trim();
if (!key) fail('POLY_PIZZA_API_KEY is missing: add it to .env (poly.pizza/settings/api).');
const headers = { 'x-auth-token': key };

async function api(p) {
  const res = await fetch(`${API}${p}`, { headers });
  if (!res.ok) fail(`${res.status} ${res.statusText} for ${p}: ${(await res.text()).slice(0, 200)}`);
  return res.json();
}
function fail(msg) { console.error(`✗ ${msg}`); process.exit(1); }
const line = (m) => `${m.ID}  ${m.Title}  ·  ${m.Licence}  ·  ${m['Tri Count']} tris${m.Animated ? '  ·  animated' : ''}  ·  ${m.Creator?.Username}`;

if (cmd === 'search') {
  const q = new URLSearchParams({ Limit: opt('limit') ?? '10' });
  if (flag('cc0')) q.set('License', '1');
  if (opt('category')) q.set('Category', opt('category'));
  if (flag('animated')) q.set('Animated', '1');
  const term = args.join(' ');
  if (!term && q.size < 2) fail('search needs a keyword or a filter (--cc0, --category n, --animated).');
  const { total, results } = await api(`/search${term ? `/${encodeURIComponent(term)}` : ''}?${q}`);
  console.log(`${total} found; showing ${results.length}:`);
  for (const m of results) console.log(`  ${line(m)}`);
} else if (cmd === 'get') {
  if (!args[0]) fail('get needs a model id.');
  const m = await api(`/model/${args[0]}`);
  console.log(line(m));
  console.log(`  ${m.Attribution}`);
  console.log(`  thumbnail: ${m.Thumbnail}`);
} else if (cmd === 'download') {
  if (!args[0]) fail('download needs a model id.');
  const m = await api(`/model/${args[0]}`);
  const name = (args[1] || m.Title).toLowerCase().replace(/[^a-z0-9]+/g, '-').replace(/^-|-$/g, '');
  const res = await fetch(m.Download, { headers });
  const body = Buffer.from(await res.arrayBuffer());
  if (!res.ok || body.subarray(0, 4).toString() !== 'glTF') fail(`download failed (${res.status}); not a GLB (Cloudflare may block datacenter IPs).`);
  const dir = path.join(root, 'public', 'assets', 'models');
  await mkdir(dir, { recursive: true });

## User · 13:28:57

Base directory for this skill: /root/.claude/skills/synced/073f8787-084e-430d-abff-4e48188623f3_63e77896-e25f-4a80-a673-40f6fab94743/built-in-browser

# Built-in browser

The built-in browser is a real browser pane inside the Claude desktop app, separate from the person's Chrome. Its tools are named `mcp__Claude_Browser__*` when the session itself runs inside the desktop app, and `mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser__*` when the session runs in the cloud (started from the web, a phone, or the desktop app) and is linked to the person's computer. The names after the prefix are the same either way, Claude uses whichever prefix is actually present, and this skill refers to the tools by the part after the prefix.

If the only built-in browser tool present is `enable__mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser`, Claude calls it first: it turns the built-in browser on for this conversation, and the `mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser__*` tools appear once it has run.

## What the person can see

The browser pane shares the desktop app's side panel with artifacts, documents, and file previews, and the panel shows one of them at a time. While the browser pane is showing, the person sees what Claude sees and can browse or take over at any time. While something else is open in the panel, or the panel is closed, the built-in browser keeps working but the person cannot see it.

Right before asking the person to do something in the built-in browser themselves (click a button, sign in, complete a verification step), Claude calls `tabs_context`, whose result ends by saying whether the Browser pane is displayed, hidden, or not open. If the pane is not open, Claude opens the page first and checks again. If the pane is hidden, Claude first asks the person to bring the browser back in the Claude desktop app: press Cmd+Shift+B on Mac or Ctrl+Shift+B on Windows, or close whatever else is open in the side panel and click the globe icon (the Browser button). Claude then says what to do in the browser. Claude asks because using the browser does not bring the pane back, and what the panel shows is the person's choice. Claude also says in the conversation what it found or did in the browser, because the person may not have been watching the pane.

## Sign-ins persist, and they are the person's

The built-in browser keeps its own persistent profile, shared across the desktop app's sessions. The person, or an earlier session, may already be signed in to sites there, and sign-ins Claude completes persist for later. Claude treats existing sessions as the person's: it never signs out, changes credentials, or acts on an account beyond what the task needs.

## Tabs

The built-in browser has tabs. `preview_start` with a `url` opens an additional tab at that URL in one call and returns a `tabId`, leaving existing tabs untouched, so Claude prefers it over `tabs_create` followed by `navigate` when the destination is already known. `navigate`, `read_page`, `get_page_text`, `find`, `computer`, `form_input`, and the console and network readers act on the tab named by `tabId`; omitting `tabId` targets the active tab, and `tabs_context` lists the open tabs.

## Loading via ToolSearch

Claude loads the built-in browser tools in bulk, not one-by-one: if they are in the deferred list, Claude loads them all in a single ToolSearch call whose query is their full name prefix, for example `{ query: "mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser__", max_results: 64 }`.

## Reading pages

Claude prefers `get_page_text` and `read_page` over screenshots for reading, because they return the page's actual text and structure rather than pixels of the visible viewport. `computer` with action "screenshot" is for when the visual layout is the point or the person asks to see the page.

## Site approvals, blocked sites, and request_access

Depending on the person's approval settings, the person may be asked to approve a site before Claude acts on it, and some sites are blocked outright. Claude waits for a pending approval rather than working around it. If a page is refused or an approval is declined, Claude tells the person and moves on rather than retrying.

When the tools carry the `mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser__` prefix, approvals can be answered from any of the person's devices and may take a moment to arrive, and the session may also have a `request_access` tool. If a browser tool answers that the site is not allowed yet and `request_access` is present, Claude calls it with that site's URL and scope "once" (or "site" when the task will keep using that site), waits for the person's answer, and then retries the original tool. Without `request_access`, a refused site is handled as above: Claude tells the person and moves on.

## What the built-in browser cannot open

The built-in browser cannot open `file://` URLs or `localhost` servers that Claude starts itself, because those run where Claude's shell runs, which is not where the browser pane runs. To show the person HTML that Claude generated, Claude uses an artifact instead.

## When the built-in browser cannot be reached

When the tools carry the `mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser__` prefix, the pane runs in the Claude desktop app on the person's computer while Claude runs elsewhere, so it is reachable only while that app is open and online. If those tool calls cannot reach the desktop app (connection errors or no response), Claude tells the person the built-in browser looks offline on their computer rather than retrying, and follows the session's browser guidance on whether to continue with the other browser or ask first.

# Handwritten notes (2026-11-04)

The three photos are the owner's original notes. Summary:

**Page 1 — screen flow:** game icon + simple name + easy URL → splash screen with
the emblem → loading screen with music, animation, a progress bar with haptics
that keeps creeping so it never looks frozen → main menu (title XOR logo, a big
PLAY button, at most 2 other actions, 3 small icon buttons at the bottom) →
tutorial level.

**Page 2 — tutorial rules:** immediate access back to the menu, no bloat, zero
text (symbols only), teach movement gestures first, give the player something to
own and something to be responsible for, a reward based on performance; they
don't need to learn everything, just enough to start. The empty box under the
tutorial is the core loop, decided by the brief.

**Page 3 — the flow:**
- *Start:* context, receive the email, decompile it into a `.md`, list
  deliverables, make a timeline, set targets.
- *Setup:* find a skill, an integration and a library for each deliverable.
- *Plan:* show different designs in Figma; different designs per page.
- *Grind:* step-by-step plan, one page at a time, bare-bones pages first.
- *Test:* no bugs, core loop works, deploy and connect.
- *Polish:* remove AI fluff, play the game, add haptics and music.
- *Retention:* keep user data, customisable items, grindable items, levels and
  rewards.

How these feed into the workflow: see
`processes/assessment-day.md`.

**Done and checked**
- **Cloudflare:** created the D1 database `infinitytelemetry` (Western Europe) and an R2 bucket called `infinity-assets`. Both are on the same account as your `infinity-games-interview` Worker. I added them to `wrangler.jsonc` as `DB` and `BUCKET`, and a test deploy with them passed.
- **Live site:** `infinity.chitswa.com` loads the "Coming soon" page in a real browser. I also changed the live URL in `PLAN.md` to it, with workers.dev as the fallback.
- **Figma:** connected as your student account. Your seat still shows "View", so I tested a write: I created a blank file called "Infinity — design directions" and it worked. That rules out the seat problem for writes. Reads still count against your 200 a day.
- **ElevenLabs:** connected, and sound effects, music and voice generation are available. You don't need a key in `.env` for this.

**Needs you (about 5 minutes)**
1. **GitHub:** sign in in the Claude browser pane. It's open on github.com/new. Tell me when you're in and I'll create `infinity` as a private repo. Then I'll point your folder's `origin` at it; it currently points to `InfinityGamesInterviewWorkspace`.
2. **Bot protection:** sites that aren't a full browser still get blocked on `infinity.chitswa.com`. My fetch tool was refused, and your deploy notes say the smoke test hits the same challenge. Your notes already say to fix this before submitting. In the Cloudflare dashboard, go to chitswa.com → Security → Bots and turn off Bot Fight Mode. I left it alone because it's a security setting on your account.
3. **Checks only your computer can run:** this session has no terminal on your machine. Run these in the folder:
   ```
   npx wrangler whoami
   npm run check:pre-window
   agy --version
   npm run models -- search car
   ```
   - `whoami` should show the MalvinCF account.
   - `agy --version` should say 1.5.2. If it doesn't, your notes say to run one small probe task first.
   - The last command tests your Poly Pizza key. The key is in `.env`, but my sandbox can't reach poly.pizza.
   
   Also open Antigravity on this folder and say "claude has work for you". It should reply "No tasks for me."

**Before you go private, two things to know**
- Your brief lists the prompt log as "`logs/` on GitHub". A private repo means the assessors can't open it. You can add them as collaborators, make it public after the security check, or attach the logs to the email.
- Your runbook says to start a **fresh** Claude Code session when the window opens, so the log export is clean. Use a new session for the build, not this one.

I won't add AI co-author lines to commits, as `AGENTS.md` asks.

# Prompt log (required deliverable)

The log must be **complete and unedited**, from **every** AI tool used, and it must
include the general AI rules and system-prompt files the agents worked with.
Messy is fine; missing is not. Everything goes into `logs/` and is committed, so
one GitHub link opens it all.

**One command at the freeze: `npm run logs`** (= `logs:claude` + `logs:worker` +
`logs:rules`). The sections below explain each part.

## 1. Rules and system-prompt files

`npm run logs:rules` copies into `logs/rules/`: `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, the
Antigravity rule file, every skill, the MCP configs, Claude's user settings and
auto-memory for this folder, and Antigravity CLI settings (values that look like
keys are redacted). Run it at the end, so it captures the final state.

## 2. Claude Code

`npm run logs:claude` exports every Claude Code session that ran in this folder
to `logs/claude/` as readable Markdown plus the raw `.jsonl` (the complete record,
including tool calls and results). The npm script exports only sessions with
activity from **2026-11-07 13:30** on (owner's decision: setup work is not part of
the log). So **start a fresh Claude
Code session in this folder on the day** — a reused preparation session would be
exported whole. Every value in `.env` is replaced with `[redacted NAME]` in both
files, and the export fails if one survives. Sub-agent work appears inside the session transcript.

## 3. Gemini worker (Antigravity CLI)

`npm run worker` saves, for every task: the exact prompt and allow-list, the full
event stream (`.ndjson`) and a readable transcript (`.md`) with every tool call,
output and reply. `npm run logs:worker` then copies Antigravity's own raw record
(conversation database and brain folder) into `logs/worker/raw/`.
Fallback exporter: the community `antigravity-history` tool.

## 4. Anything else

Antigravity IDE chats (the default worker): its Export button for each chat, saved
to `logs/worker/ide/`, or `antigravity-history`, or screenshots as a last resort.
The mailbox `claude antigravity and others/` (every task and report) is committed
too — it is the readable record of each hand-off. Figma MCP calls appear in the Claude log. ElevenLabs
generations: list prompts in `design/specs/audio.md`.

## Check before sending

- Open one exported file per tool and confirm the first prompt of the day is there.
- Search `logs/` for anything that looks like a key or token; redact if found.
- In the email, list each tool and where its log is.

// Export every Claude Code session that ran in this folder to readable Markdown,
// plus the raw .jsonl (the complete, unedited record) — for the prompt-log deliverable.
//   npm run logs:claude                 → all sessions for this folder
//   npm run logs:claude -- --since 2026-11-07T14:00
// Output: logs/claude/<date>_<session>.md and .jsonl
import { readdir, readFile, writeFile, mkdir } from 'node:fs/promises';
import os from 'node:os';
import path from 'node:path';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';

const root = path.resolve(path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), '..');
const projects = path.join(os.homedir(), '.claude', 'projects');
const out = path.join(root, 'logs', 'claude');
const sinceArg = process.argv.indexOf('--since');
const since = sinceArg > 0 ? new Date(process.argv[sinceArg + 1]) : null;
const MAX_TOOL_CHARS = 6000; // the .jsonl keeps everything; Markdown stays readable

const secrets = (await readFile(path.join(root, '.env'), 'utf8').catch(() => ''))
  .split(/\r?\n/).map((l) => l.match(/^\s*([A-Z0-9_]+)\s*=\s*["']?([^"'\s#]{8,})/)).filter(Boolean).map((m) => [m[1], m[2]]);
const norm = (p) => path.resolve(p).toLowerCase();
const inThisFolder = (cwd) => cwd && (norm(cwd) === norm(root) || norm(cwd).startsWith(norm(root) + path.sep));

const files = [];
for (const dir of await readdir(projects, { withFileTypes: true }).catch(() => [])) {
  if (!dir.isDirectory()) continue;
  for (const f of await readdir(path.join(projects, dir.name)).catch(() => [])) {
    if (f.endsWith('.jsonl')) files.push(path.join(projects, dir.name, f));
  }
}

await mkdir(out, { recursive: true });
let exported = 0;

for (const file of files) {
  const lines = (await readFile(file, 'utf8')).split('\n').filter(Boolean);
  const entries = lines.map((l) => { try { return JSON.parse(l); } catch { return null; } }).filter(Boolean);
  if (!entries.some((e) => inThisFolder(e.cwd))) continue;
  const turns = entries.filter((e) => (e.type === 'user' || e.type === 'assistant') && e.message && !e.isSidechain);
  if (since && !turns.some((e) => new Date(e.timestamp) >= since)) continue;
  if (!turns.length) continue;

  const session = path.basename(file, '.jsonl');
  const first = turns[0].timestamp?.slice(0, 16).replace(/[:T]/g, '-') ?? 'unknown';
  const md = [`# Claude Code session ${session}`, '',
    `- Folder: \`${turns.find((e) => e.cwd)?.cwd ?? ''}\``,
    `- Started: ${turns[0].timestamp ?? '?'} · Ended: ${turns.at(-1).timestamp ?? '?'}`,
    `- Raw, unedited record: \`${session}.jsonl\` (same folder)`, ''];

  for (const e of turns) {
    const time = e.timestamp ? e.timestamp.slice(11, 19) : '';
    const content = typeof e.message.content === 'string' ? [{ type: 'text', text: e.message.content }] : e.message.content ?? [];
    for (const block of content) {
      if (block.type === 'text' && block.text?.trim()) {
        md.push(`## ${e.type === 'user' ? 'User' : 'Claude'} · ${time}`, '', block.text.trim(), '');
      } else if (block.type === 'image') {
        md.push(`## User · ${time}`, '', '_[image attached]_', '');
      } else if (block.type === 'thinking' && block.thinking?.trim()) {
        md.push('<details><summary>Claude thinking</summary>', '', block.thinking.trim(), '', '</details>', '');
      } else if (block.type === 'tool_use') {
        md.push(`**Tool call · ${block.name}** (${time})`, '', '```json', clip(JSON.stringify(block.input, null, 2)), '```', '');

  await writeFile(path.join(out, `${first}_${session}.md`), scrub(md.join('\n')));
  // The raw record is copied byte for byte except for secrets (see scrub below).
  await writeFile(path.join(out, `${first}_${session}.jsonl`), scrub(lines.join('\n') + '\n'));
  exported++;
}

// Final guard: no .env value may survive anywhere in logs/claude/.
for (const f of await readdir(out)) {
  const text = await readFile(path.join(out, f), 'utf8');
  const leak = secrets.find(([, v]) => text.includes(v));
  if (leak) { console.error(`✗ ${leak[0]} still present in logs/claude/${f} — do not commit.`); process.exit(1); }
}

console.log(exported
  ? `Exported ${exported} Claude Code session(s) to logs/claude/ (Markdown + raw .jsonl).`
  : `No Claude Code sessions found for ${root}${since ? ` since ${since.toISOString()}` : ''}.`);

// Secrets: every value in the git-ignored .env is replaced literally (a key pasted
// into chat is otherwise in the transcript), plus anything shaped like key=value.
function scrub(text) {
  for (const [name, value] of secrets) text = text.split(value).join(`[redacted ${name}]`);
  return text.replace(/((?:api[_-]?key|auth[_-]?token|secret|password|authorization)[\\"'\s:=]+)[A-Za-z0-9_\-.]{16,}/gi, '$1[redacted]');
}

function clip(s) {
  return s.length > MAX_TOOL_CHARS ? `${s.slice(0, MAX_TOOL_CHARS)}\n… [${s.length - MAX_TOOL_CHARS} more characters in the raw .jsonl]` : s;
}
```

# The studio and the market (research, 2026-11-06)

Preparation notes for choosing and judging a prototype. Facts were gathered by the
Gemini worker and checked by Claude against primary sources (store pages, cited
interviews); unverified numbers were left out.

## Infinity Games, in brief

- Portuguese mobile studio in Odivelas, Lisbon; founder & CEO Muhammad Satar; roughly
  40 people. Positioning: relaxing, minimal games that reduce stress.
- 250M+ downloads claimed in an October 2024 interview (PocketGamer.biz, marketing lead André
  Miraldo); LinkedIn states 130M+ (older).
- Confirmed on Google Play (developer "Infinity Games, Lda"): Infinity Loop, Loop Hex,
  Loop Energy, Shapes, Linea, Harmony, Pipes, Chill, Connection, One Line, Laser Quest,
  Fireballz, Square Paint, Eureka, Furzies, Solitaire Plus, Spider Solitaire, Railways,
  Traffix, Package Inc, Wood Blocks 3D, Inspector Meow, Enigmic, plus a
  growing line of idle/tycoon games (Idle Dot Shooter, Broke to Boss, Ranch Legacy, Idle Truck Fleet,
  Flight Manager, Freightbound, Golf Inc Tycoon, Hometown Express, Voltara, Glint Core,
  Ricochet TD, Mayday Hero).

## Style guide — what the flagship puzzles share

Drawn from the core puzzle line (Infinity Loop, Hex, Energy, Shapes, Pipes, Linea,
Harmony, Connection):

- **One input.** Mostly single taps (rotate a piece, toggle a state) or one smooth drag.
- **No failure.** No timers, no lives, no move penalties; you explore until the board
  settles. Their simulation games add pressure, but retries are instant and local.
- **Quiet visuals.** Dark or soft single-colour canvases, thin luminous lines or soft
  pastel shapes, no clutter; the solved state is visibly beautiful.
- **Music as reward.** Gentle tones and chords on progress and completion, ASMR-like
  clicks; sound confirms rather than alarms.
- **Monetisation is light-touch.** Ads between levels, rewarded hints, a "remove ads"
  purchase; nothing that gates play.

A prototype "fits" when it keeps one input, removes failure or softens it, shows a
beautiful solved state, and makes sound part of the reward.

The owner's playtest of Infinity Loop and Energy adds: make the goal legible, show what
can be touched, animate hints as nudges (never autoplay), and give a visible, cosmetic
collection reward. Details: `knowledge/playtest-insights.md`.

## The charts (Google Play Portugal, 2026-11-06)

Top free puzzle titles and their verified store facts:

| # | Game | Core loop | Rating · downloads |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Meowdoku | Place one cat per row, column and region, none touching (Queens-style deduction) | 4.8 · 10M+ |
| 2 | Magic Sort! | Pour coloured layers between tubes until each holds one colour | 4.5 · 10M+ |
| 3 | Rotate Rings | Rotate interlocking rings until they come apart / align | 4.7 · 10M+ |
| 4 | Amaze GO! | Tap pieces so each slides out along its arrow without colliding | 4.8 · 100M+ |
| 9 | Word Tiles | Clear letter tiles by forming words | 4.9 · 1M+ |

The top free chart games sell the same calm promise as Infinity, but add hearts (a
light fail state) and heavy marketing (see `knowledge/playtest-insights.md`).

Top grossing is dominated by match-3 + meta (Candy Crush Saga, Royal Match,
Gardenscapes, Gossip Harbor's merge-2) and by scarcity-driven designs (Coin Master,
MONOPOLY GO) — the latter are counter-examples for a calm catalogue.

## Shortlist for a calm prototype (if the brief leaves the choice open)

Scored on provable in ~2 hours, fit with the style guide, current trend, twist
potential and risk. #1 is the owner's choice (2026-11-06) despite carrying the most risk:

1. **Hover Rush** (Infinity's own new release; one-thumb 3D hoverboard racing) — the
   strongest showcase: game feel, pacing and the studio's newest direction. Core to
   prove: hold to race, steer, gold ramp → **RUSH** slow-motion stunt → speed burst →
   podium in about a minute. Build approach: pseudo-3D road first (`knowledge/fast-3d.md`),
   three.js only if real 3D is essential; build the RUSH moment first; desktop controls
   (arrows/mouse to steer, Space to hop, a key/click for RUSH). Calm, Infinity-style
   framing: no crashes that end a run, minimal visuals, the stunt as the earned reward.
   **Risk:** highest of the list — camera feel, speed and rivals in ~90 minutes; fall
   back to ghost rivals and one track if time runs short. See
   `knowledge/playtest-insights.md` → Hover Rush.
2. **Amaze GO!** — one input, pure geometry, charting now. Calm twist: no lives;
   collisions bounce back softly; each piece that leaves plays a note, so a clean solve
   becomes a phrase of music.
3. **Magic Sort!** — universally understood and calm already. Twist: layers of light
   that ring like tuned glass when a tube completes; unlimited undo.
4. **Meowdoku** — deduction with a clean visual grammar. Twist: monochrome regions,
   gentle conflict glows, a reveal on completion. Hand-author a few unique-solution
   grids; keep hints minimal (finding the move is the game).
5. **Rotate Rings** — closest to Infinity Loop, so the twist must justify another
   rotation game (e.g. rings as sound loops that phase into rhythm when aligned).

In reserve: **Match-3** (Candy Crush; Puzzle & Dragons' free orb-drag as the variant) —
the most familiar genre and a large scope; twist: no move limit, orb-drag, an earned
finale (principles §4–6).

Whatever is chosen, it is built fresh inside the window.

---
name: game-brainstorm
description: Generate strong, studio-fitting game concepts fast when time pressure is killing creativity — diverge with proven idea generators, filter hard against the brief, the studio's style and a two-hour build, then present a ranked handful the owner can pick from in minutes. Use when choosing what to build, when looking for the twist on an existing game, or whenever the owner says they're stuck.
---

# Game brainstorm

Pressure makes people (and models) grab the first idea. This skill forces a wide,
structured search first and a ruthless filter second, so the owner only has to *choose*.
The owner decides; Claude generates, filters and recommends.

## Inputs (read first, 1 minute)

`context/brief.md` (what's asked), `knowledge/market-and-studio.md` (style guide,
shortlist), `knowledge/playtest-insights.md` (what the owner loved and hated),
`knowledge/game-design-principles.md`. If the brief names a game or theme, it constrains
everything below.

## The quality bar

The owner's two reference games set the bar:
- **Energy** — instant clarity: glowing affordances, a two-move tutorial, a visible
  collection reward.
- **Linea** — meaning and connection: a visible goal, a tiny story carried by the
  mechanic itself, every animation and sound coming from the player's one gesture.
A concept that couldn't sit beside these two is not done yet.

## 1. Diverge (10 minutes, at least 12 raw ideas)

Write ideas as one line each: **verb + world + feeling**. Use every generator at least
once; quantity first, no judging yet.

| Generator | Prompt to yourself |
| --- | --- |
| **Remove the stress** | Take the inspiration's core loop; delete its timer, hearts or fail state. What becomes interesting instead? |
| **Swap the verb** | Same goal, different input: tap → drag → trace → hold → rotate → flick. (Puzzle & Dragons is match-3 with a drag.) |
| **Make it sound** | What if every action plays a note and a solved board is a finished phrase of music? |
| **Make it light** | What if the pieces were light, glass, water, thread, ink or sand — and solving made it beautiful? |
| **Give it a story** | What tiny wordless story could the mechanic tell over 3–5 levels? (Linea) |
| **Connect it** | What if one action causes a visible chain of consequences across the screen? |
| **Flip the goal** | Instead of clearing, build; instead of escaping, gathering; instead of winning, settling. |
| **Shrink it** | The smallest version of a big game: one turret, one ring, one tube, one line. |
| **Collect it** | What would a calm collection look like — themed sets (constellations, glyphs, seasons)? |
| **Problem, then solution** | Which limit does the player feel first, and which single tool relieves it? (principle 14) |
| **Real-world calm** | Folding, sorting, pruning, weaving, tuning, tidying — which satisfying chore could be the loop? |
| **Cross two charts** | Combine the verb of one charting game with the goal of another. |

## 2. Filter (5 minutes) — every surviving idea must pass all five

1. **Brief:** does it do what the email asks?
2. **One input:** can you play it with one gesture plus clicks, on desktop?
3. **Two-hour core:** can the core loop be playable and fun in about 90 minutes?
4. **Studio fit:** minimal visuals, low stress, a beautiful solved state, sound as reward?
5. **The 10-second test:** would a viewer understand what to do and feel the twist
   within 10 seconds of the video?

Drop anything that fails. If fewer than three survive, run the generators again with the
failures as input ("why did it fail? what's the nearest idea that wouldn't?").

## 3. Shape the top 3–5 (5 minutes)

For each survivor, a card:

```
Name (working title):
One line: <verb> <what> to <goal>, <feeling>.
Inspiration: <chart game> — what we keep, what we remove.
The twist: <the one thing that makes it an Infinity game>.
First 30 seconds: what the player sees, does and hears.
The goal on screen: how the objective stays visible.
Problem → solution: the first limit felt and the tool that answers it.
The reward moment: what happens on solve (animation + sound).
Two-hour scope: must-have vs. nice-to-have.
Risk: the one thing most likely to make it flat.
Question it answers (for scale-or-kill):
```

## 4. Recommend

Rank them with the shortlist lenses (provable in ~2 h, fit, trend, twist, risk), mark a
recommendation, and give the owner one sentence per card. The owner picks — or combines
two cards. Log the choice and the runners-up in `PLAN.md`.

## Never

- Never present only one idea, or ideas that differ only in theme or colour.
- Never generic "AI" concepts: neon-gradient everything, sparkles without meaning,
  "relaxing" as a label instead of a mechanic.
- Never ideas that need content you can't make in two hours (big level sets, 3D, online).
- Never pressure mechanics as the hook (timers, lives, scarcity) for this studio.
- Never write game code here — this skill ends at a chosen concept.
---
name: scale-or-kill
description: Frame a game prototype as a pitch to a studio's prototype team — which game inspired it and why, what the prototype tested, what was learned, and a clear verdict to scale it or kill it with reasons. Use when choosing which game to prototype, to write the keep/kill criteria before building, and for the walkthrough video script and the written answers.
---

how it could fit the studio's style (for Infinity Games: relaxing, minimal,
polished, stress-free). Pick the one with the best mix of *provable in two hours*
and *interesting twist for this studio*. Record the choice and runners-up i

## Claude · 14:07:49

I've written the brief into `context/brief.md` (every deliverable from the email, deadline 18:00). I also saved your chart screenshots and the email in `context/charts/`, so the video can show the game was really charting today.

Your earlier first choice, Hover Rush, is out: the email says the game must be in a top chart now, and Hover Rush isn't. Here's the shortlist from today's charts, in `PLAN.md`, with my pick first:

1. **Amaze GO!** (#5 free, 100M+ downloads). Tap a piece and it slides out along its arrow; clear the board.
   - **The twist:** no lives, so a blocked piece just bumps back softly, and each piece that leaves plays a note. A clean solve sounds like a short tune.
   - **Why it's strong:** it sells exactly Infinity's promise ("calm a busy mind"), it's simple to build, and levels can be generated backwards so they're always solvable.
   - **Risk:** the least spectacular of the four.
2. **Paper.io 2** (#16 free). Leave your area, loop back, and the land you enclosed becomes yours.
   - **The twist:** you draw with a line of light, like Linea, and each area you claim fills in more of a hidden picture. Nobody can kill you.
   - **Why it's strong:** the best-looking result, and it shows game feel.
   - **Risk:** the area fill has to feel good in 75 minutes.
3. **Block Blast!** (#9 free, 4.8★). Place shapes on a grid; full rows and columns clear.
   - **Why it's strong:** the safest build.
   - **Risk:** hard to make it not look like a copy.
4. **Subway Surfers** (#23 free). A runner, the closest thing to your Hover Rush wish.
   - **The twist:** hitting something slows you down instead of ending the run.
   - **Risk:** the highest, because so much depends on the 3D feel.

---

❓ **Q1 – Which game?** Pick one, or combine two.

➡️ Amaze GO! with the musical, no-lives twist. It has the strongest scale-or-kill story: the market already pays for calm, so does the calm version still hold attention without hearts? Paper.io 2 is the one to take if you want the wow factor and accept more risk.

---

❓ **Q2 – How to deliver?** The email allows one email or one Notion/Word page.

➡️ One reply email with five sections: link, video, prompt log, answers, and a one-line verdict. The prompt log goes in as a link to a readable export.

---

❓ **Q3 – How strict on the clock?** The email suggests 15/75/15/15 minutes, roughly a 16:00 finish. Your runbook says complete by 16:00, polish until 17:00, then freeze.

➡️ Keep the runbook: complete by 16:00, freeze at 17:00, send by about 17:30.

Answer "yes" to accept all three, or reply only to the ones you disagree with. Once the game is settled, I'll lock the plan and start the three visual directions in Figma.

# Plan

Read `AGENTS.md`, `context/brief.md`, the current `PLAN.md`, `TASKS.md` and the
relevant `processes/` file first. State the outcome in one sentence.

## Write the plan around deliverables, not tool calls

1. **Deliverables.** List every deliverable the brief asks for, including
   non-code ones (recording, logs, written answers, email). Nothing the brief
   asks for may be missing.
2. **Contract.** Lock the shared interfaces before parallel work: engine
   functions, event names, the save-data schema, file ownership. Two agents must
   never edit the same file; say who owns each.
3. **Milestones with a time and a check.** Each milestone names its output and the
   observation that proves it (a passing command, a screen matching its frame, a
   URL loading on the phone). Use the assessment-day times when they apply.
4. **Mandatory vs optional, ranked and gated.** Separate what makes the game
   complete from polish and extensions. Rank every optional item by one test:
   **will the assessor see it in the video or the first two minutes of play?**
   Seen items (look, feel, sound, tutorial) come first; unseen ones (server
   telemetry, backends, accounts, leaderboards, consent banners) come last and
   usually become a Q6 answer instead. Give each item a **time gate** written now
   ("only if the freeze checklist is green by 16:45"), so nobody decides mid-flow.
   Fill the "Optional" table in `PLAN.md`. When time is short, cut mechanics
   before visuals.
5. **Worker tasks.** Write every bounded, non-visual job as a task file in
   `claude antigravity and others/tasks/` (format in that folder's README) and
   add a row to `TASKS.md`. Tasks that touch disjoint files can run in parallel —
   label them `[parallel]`.

## Ask only what changes the work

Ask the owner only for decisions that change the outcome; give a recommended
answer for each (use `grill-me` for anything larger). Look facts up yourself.
Continue preparing independent work while an answer is pending.

## Keep it true

Update `PLAN.md` when evidence changes the approach (log it in the decision
table with the time). Never mark a milestone done without its check. Keep the
file resumable: a fresh context window must be able to continue from it alone.
For handing work to another agent, use `feature-handoff`.
# Fast 3D (and fake 3D) for a two-hour browser prototype

General techniques, no game code. Choose the cheapest technique that sells the feeling;
players judge speed, impact and clarity, not polygon counts. Design rules for racing,
runner and arcade games (camera, boost, collisions, rivals, divergence):
`fast-racing-principles.md`.

## 1. Pick the technique

| Technique | Looks like | Cost in 2 h | Use when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Pseudo-3D road** (Canvas 2D) | Classic arcade racers: a road rushing toward you, curves, hills, scaled sprites | Low — a few hundred lines, no library | Racing, runners, anything moving forward fast |
| **Pre-rendered sprites + parallax** (Canvas 2D) | Rich "3D" characters on a 2D stage (how Chicken Invaders looks so full) | Low | Shooters, arcade, anything with a fixed camera |
| **Real 3D, low-poly** (three.js) | Modern 3D with fog, glow and camera motion | Medium–high — camera feel is the risk | When depth or rotation is the point, or the brief asks for 3D |

Default for a racer under time pressure: **pseudo-3D**. Default for "it must be real 3D":
three.js with a locked camera and simple shapes.

## 2. Pseudo-3D road, in principle

- The road is a list of segments ahead of the camera. Each segment has a curve value
  and a height.
- Each frame, project every segment from far to near: screen scale is
  *camera depth ÷ distance*, so far segments are thin and high on screen, near ones wide
  and low. Draw each as a trapezoid (road, rumble strips, grass) in alternating shades —
  the alternation is what reads as speed.
- **Curves:** accumulate each segment's curve as a sideways offset, so the road bends.
  Push the player sideways on curves (centrifugal pull) to make steering matter.
- **Hills:** segment heights shift the projected y; the horizon line moves with them.
- **Sprites** (rivals, ramps, scenery) sit on segments and scale with the same factor;
  draw them far to near so near ones cover far ones.
- **Speed feel:** field of view widens slightly with speed, segment stripes blur past,
  the camera shakes faintly at top speed.
- Styled minimal (flat colours, fog-gradient sky, glowing road edges) it reads as a
  deliberate look, not retro.

## 3. Pre-rendered sprites and parallax

- 3D models rendered into images once (or simple vector shapes) and drawn as flat
  sprites; fixed camera; several background layers moving at different speeds.
- Collisions as circles; many cheap particles; reuse objects from a pool instead of
  creating new ones every frame.

## 4. Real 3D with three.js (when you must)

- Load three.js from a CDN through an import map (no build step); see the
  `threejs-fundamentals` skill.
- **Low-poly + fog + one light** carries a style fast; bloom from the post-processing
  skill gives cheap glow. Flat colours beat textures in two hours.
- **Lock the camera** behind and above the player; smooth its follow (lerp), never let
  it collide with scenery. Camera feel makes or breaks it — build it first.
- **No physics engine** for a racer prototype: move along a spline or lanes with scripted
  speed curves; rivals follow the same track with gentle rubber-banding.
- Instancing for repeated scenery (`threejs-performance`); keep draw calls low.

## 5. The signature moment (RUSH, combo, finale) — in any engine

The moment players remember is a short sequence, not a feature:
1. **Anticipation** — a readable cue (gold ram

## Claude · 14:17:28

I don't have to install anything. Your skills, `plan`, `grill-me` and the rest, are in your folder, and I'm reading them from there. `grill-me` just hands over to `grilling`, which is the question-round format below.

**What I think of the idea:** it's good, and it does what you want. The skill is the route through the lanes, a puzzle decision made at running speed. That shows both the puzzle side and the arcade side of the studio. There are three things I'd fix before we lock it:

1. **The brief asks for one game's core mechanic.** So the inspiration we name should be **Subway Surfers** (#23 top free today). Its core is switching lanes while running and collecting things. The match-3 colour chain is our twist, borrowed from Candy Crush and Royal Match (#5 and #7 top grossing). I'd keep Gossip Harbor out of the pitch: it's a merge game, not match-3, and an assessor might call that out.
2. **One event currently sends two opposite signals.** Picking a different colour makes your gems explode (a reward) and also slows you down (a punishment). Players won't know whether they did something good. The fix is in Q2 below.
3. **"Three slowdowns and it's over" is a hidden counter.** If the chaser's distance is your life bar instead, players can see the danger. It also lets them win distance back, which is where the comeback tension comes from.

---

❓ **Q1 – Reference game:** shall we pitch Subway Surfers (Google Play top free #23, 07/11) as the inspiration, with the match-3 colour chain as the twist?

➡️ Yes.

---

❓ **Q2 – The colour-chain rule:** gems fill a 5-slot bar at the top of the screen.
- **5 of the same colour:** a MATCH burst. You get big points, a short speed boost and the chaser falls back, and the bar empties so you can start any colour for free.
- **A different colour before 5:** the chain breaks. Your gems pop for small points (count × 10), you stumble, and the chaser gains. The new gem starts the next chain.
- **Combos:** MATCH bursts in a row multiply your score (×2, ×3…).

➡️ Yes. A full chain is always good and breaking early always costs you, so the meaning is never mixed.

---

❓ **Q3 – How the run fails:** the chaser's distance is shown on screen as your life bar. Breaking a chain and hitting a barrier both close the gap, MATCH bursts open it, and the run ends when you're caught. Should this replace "3 slowdowns and it's over"?

➡️ Yes. You can see the danger, you can recover, and it gives the comeback moments that look good in a video.

---

❓ **Q4 – How it looks in 3D:** real low-poly 3D in three.js, with a fixed camera behind the runner, three lanes, fog and glowing gems.

➡️ Yes, three.js, because "go big" means real 3D for the video. My notes from earlier prep (`knowledge/fast-3d.md`) say a fake-3D road drawn in 2D is cheaper. If the 3D isn't playable by **15:15**, I'll switch to that, written down as a deadline now.

---

❓ **Q5 – Controls and obstacles:**
- **Controls:** ← → or A/D to change lanes (or click the left or right half of the screen), and Space to jump. Space/Enter starts, Esc pauses.
- **Obstacles:** a few low barriers to jump over, from about 20 seconds in. Before that, choosing a colour is the only challenge.

➡️ Yes, and if time gets short, jumping is the first thing to cut.

---

❓ **Q6 – What ends a run and what counts:** one endless run that gets faster over time, with your score and best score shown. No levels.

➡️ Yes. A run lasts about 1–3 minutes, so the video can show a whole one.

---

Answer "yes" to accept everything, or reply only to the ones you disagree with. The next round covers the chaser (what it is and how it shows on screen), the world theme, and three visual directions in Figma. Then I lock `PLAN.md` and start the code parts that don't touch the visuals.

## Claude · 14:25:00

I've logged everything you've settled so far in the decision table in `PLAN.md`: fake 3D, jump but no slide, the background changing over the run, and the chaser as your life bar.

On Q4: three.js doesn't need Blender or Unity. It's code, plus free models from Poly Pizza, which your key already works for. Fake 3D first is the right call anyway. Moving up to real 3D later is realistic if we keep the game rules separate from the drawing code, which your setup already does. The swap then only replaces the part that draws.

**"A game first, pretty later" runs into your own design gate.** `AGENTS.md` says no screens or visuals until you've picked a look; you wrote that rule after the rehearsal. Here's how we keep both:
- **Right now, no visuals needed:** I build the game rules with tests (lanes, gems, chains, scoring, chaser, speed). That's about 70% of the game, so you lose no time.
- **In parallel, the fast path:** two visual directions in about 10 minutes instead of three. You pick one and the screens get built in that style from the start, so we don't make anything ugly and redo it.

If you'd rather drop the gate today, tell me and I'll log it as your decision. My recommendation is the fast path.

---

❓ **Q2b – Scoring.** This is how I read your rule, so check it:

| Chain when it ends | Points | Effect |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1–2 | 0 | stumble, the chaser gains |
| 3 | 100 | none |
| 4 | 200 (×2) | none |
| 5 | 300 (×3) | none |
| 6 (max) | 400 (×4), bursts automatically | the chaser falls back a step |

A chain ends when you pick up a different colour, which also starts the next chain, or when it reaches 6. The bar at the top has 6 slots.

➡️ Yes, including no stumble when you cash in 3 or more. Otherwise cashing in a good chain would still feel like a punishment.

---

❓ **Q7 – The chaser:** a dark storm or shadow wave that rolls in behind you, shown as a glow at the bottom of the screen and a distance meter.

➡️ Yes. It's readable, it's cheap in fake 3D, and it isn't a character you'd need to animate.

---

❓ **Q8 – World and colours:** a neon-lit road through changing settings (dusk city → desert → night sky), with 4 gem colours that are easy to tell apart, each with its own shape for colour-blind players.

➡️ Yes. We'll set the exact look in the two design directions.

---

❓ **Q9 – Work split:**
- **Me:** game rules and tests now, then the two directions in Figma.
- **The Antigravity worker:** a list of sound effects and the test cases.
- **You:** say "claude has work for you" to Antigravity when I ask.

➡️ Yes.

# Game scaffold (recipe, not code)

The workspace deliberately ships with **no game code**: everything game-related is
written inside the window. This skill lets you rebuild the proven structure fast.
Write it fresh for the chosen game; do not paste code from earlier prototypes.

## Inputs

`context/brief.md` (core loop, save-data schema), the contract in `PLAN.md`
(engine API, event names, screens) and the five-line prototype brief from
`scale-or-kill` (question, core action, keep if, kill if, gate). If the contract
isn't locked, lock it first.

## Files to create (in this order)

**Two stages.** Stage 1 (logic: `config.js`, `rng.js`, `engine.js`, `storage.js`,
`audio.js`, tests) can start as soon as the contract is locked, in parallel with
`design-first`. **Stage 2 (UI: `screens.js`, `view.js`, `main.js`, `index.html`,
`debug.js`, CSS) waits for the design gate** — the owner's pick locked in `PLAN.md`,
`design/tokens.css` and reference frames (`AGENTS.md`). A hook blocks stage-2 writes
before that; don't build a "temporary" UI to see it running — run the engine tests
instead.

| File | Responsibility | Must not |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `public/src/config.js` | Frozen `CONFIG`: every tuning number (sizes, speeds, timings, scoring, colours that are gameplay-relevant) | contain logic |
| `public/src/rng.js` | Seeded PRNG (e.g. mulberry32): `next(seed) → { value, seed }` | use `Math.random` |
| `public/src/engine.js` | Pure rules. `createGame(config, seed, opts) → state`; `step(state, action) → { state, events }`; `tick(state, dtMs) → { state, events }` (only if time matters). Events are plain objects (`{ type: 'clear', lines: 2 }`) | touch DOM, timers, storage, `Math.random`, `Date` |
| `public/src/storage.js` | Versioned save data in localStorage: `load/save/update/reset`, `export const STORAGE_KEY`, never throws (in-memory fallback), migrates unknown versions | throw on blocked storage |
| `public/src/audio.js` | `unlock()` on first click/key; `load(map)` for samples; `play(name, { pitch, volume })` with slight random pitch on repeats; procedural fallback via ZzFX (soft settings) for any missing sample; respects a mute setting | autoplay before a gesture |
| `public/src/screens.js` | `show(name)` toggles `section[data-screen]`; current screen stored on `<body data-current>` | put `data-screen` on `<body>` (it would hide the whole page) |
| `public/src/view.js` | Renders state (DOM or canvas), reacts to events with animation; reads design tokens | change engine state |
| `public/src/main.js` | Controller: input → `step` → events → view/audio/storage; rAF loop with clamped `dt`; time-based games step the engine at a fixed simulation step through an accumulator (render rate never changes the outcome); keyboard (Space/Enter start or restart, Esc pause, arrows/WASD if the game needs them); exposes `window.__GAME__` | contain rules |
| `public/index.html` | One `section[data-screen]` per screen, `<link rel="icon" href="data:,">`, one `<script type="importmap">` pinning exact versions from jsDelivr, module script | load anything from localhost; use `@latest` or unpinned versions |
| `public/src/debug.js` | Only when the URL has `?debug`: a lil-gui panel bound to the `CONFIG` numbers (feel presets, speeds, timings) for live tuning; copy good values back into `config.js` | load or show anything without `?debug` |
| `tests/engine.test.mjs` | `node:test` + `node:assert/strict`: rules, win/lose, scoring, determinism (same seed → same game), 2–3 "golden seeds" whose outcome is pinned; for races, a scripted safe line reaches the finish | need a browser |

Libraries (exact versions and URLs: `knowledge/production-toolkit.md` §2): lil-gui
for `debug.js`, ZzFX for the audio fallback, three.js only for real 3D. Tweens are
CSS transitions plus a few easing functions by default; add GSAP only when there is
a sequence to choreograph (menus, finale).

## Rules that keep the engine honest (board and puzzle games)

- Resolve until stable: after a move, re-apply the rules (matches, falls, fills)
  until nothing changes, emitting one event per step so the view can animate each.
- Undo restores **everything**, including the RNG seed, score and move count —
  otherwise undo becomes a free re-roll.
- The controller queues (or ignores) input while the board is resolving or
  animating; one move at a time.
- Generated or shuffled boards are verified solvable (generate-and-check, or build
  backwards from a solution); hand-authored levels get a test that solves them.
- Each of these gets a headless test where it applies.

## Never-stuck guarantee and idle hint

Unless the brief or the genre rules it out, build principle 13 of
`knowledge/game-design-principles.md` from the start — read its nuances first:
- In the engine: detect "no legal action" and resolve it (reshuffle, add a piece,
  gentle reset) so it can't happen while playing; and a pure `findHint(state)` that
  returns *a* valid, ordinary move — never the optimal one, never hard-coded
  (single-solution puzzles: a direction, not the answer).
- In the controller: an idle timer (~5 s) reset by clicks, keys and drags only, not
  hover; pulse first, then a ghost-cursor demonstration; any action clears it
  instantly; emit a `hint_shown` telemetry event; never touch the grade.
- In tests: for several seeds/levels, applying `findHint`'s move is legal (and makes
  progress where that's measurable); a fresh or reshuffled board always has a hint.

Pure: returns the value and the next seed.\nexport function next(seed) {\n  let t = (seed + 0x6d2b79f5) >>> 0;\n  let r = Math.imul(t ^ (t >>> 15), t | 1);\n  r ^= r + Math.imul(r ^ (r >>> 7), r | 61);\n  return { value: ((r ^ (r >>> 14)) >>> 0) / 4294967296, seed: t };\n}\n// Convenience for engine code that threads seed through a mutable cursor.\nexport function rngCursor(seed) {\n  const c = { seed: seed >>> 0 };\n  c.float = () => { const o = next(c.seed); c.seed = o.seed; return o.value; };\n  c.int = (lo, hi) => lo + Math.floor(c.float() * (hi - lo + 1));\n  return c;\n}\nEOF\necho ok",
  "description": "Write config and RNG modules"

'' : `<ellipse cx=\"${cx - r * 0.25}\" cy=\"${cy - r * 0.3}\" rx=\"${r * 0.22}\" ry=\"${r * 0.12}\" fill=\"#fff\" opacity=\".55\"/>`;\n  return `<g ${glow}>${shape}${shine}</g>`;\n}\n\nfunction defs() {\n  const g = Object.entries(T.gems).map(([k, c]) => svg('radialGradient', { id: `g-${k}`, cx: '35%', cy: '30%', r: '75%' },\n    `<stop offset=\"0\" stop-color=\"${c.light}\"/><stop offset=\".55\" stop-color=\"${c.base}\"/><stop offset=\"1\" stop-color=\"${c.deep}\"/>`)).join('');\n  return `<defs>${g}\n    <linearGradient id=\"sky\" x1=\"0\" y1=\"0\" x2=\"0\" y2=\"1\">${T.sky.map(([o, c]) => `<stop offset=\"${o}\" stop-color=\"${c}\"/>`).join('')}</linearGradient>\n    <linearGradient id=\"storm\" x1=\"0\" y1=\"1\" x2=\"0\" y2=\"0\"><stop offset=\"0\" stop-color=\"${T.storm.core}\"/><stop offset=\".55\" stop-color=\"${T.storm.core}\" stop-opacity=\".85\"/><stop offset=\"1\" stop-color=\"${T.storm.core}\" stop-opacity=\"0\"/></linearGradient>\n    <filter id=\"glow\" x=\"-80%\" y=\"-80%\" width=\"260%\" height=\"260%\"><feGaussianBlur stdDeviation=\"${T.glow}\" result=\"b\"/><feMerge><feMergeNode in=\"b\"/><feMergeNode in=\"SourceGraphic\"/></feMerge></filter>\n    <filter id=\"soft\"><feGaussianBlur stdDeviation=\"6\"/></filter>\n  </defs>`;\n}\n\nconst HOR = H * 0.37, NEAR = H * 1.02, HALF = W * 0.72;\nconst proj = (z) => { const s = 1 / z; return { y: HOR + (NEAR - HOR) * s, s }; };\n\nfunction world(opts = {}) {\n  let out = `<rect width=\"${W}\" height=\"${H}\" fill=\"url(#sky)\"/>`;\n  out += T.scenery(W, HOR);\n  // Road bands, far to near.\n  const zs = []; for (let z = 40; z >= 1; z -= 0.5) zs.push(z);\n  for (let i = 0; i < zs.length - 1; i++) {\n    const a = proj(zs[i]), b = proj(zs[i + 1]);\n    const band = Math.floor(zs[i] * 2 + (opts.phase || 0)) % 2 === 0;\n    const ha = HALF * a.s, hb = HALF * b.s, ea = ha * 1.18, eb = hb * 1.18;\n    out += `<polygon points=\"${W / 2 - ea},${a.y} ${W / 2 + ea},${a.y} ${W / 2 + eb},${b.y} ${W / 2 - eb},${b.y}\" fill=\"${band ? T.ground[0] : T.ground[1]}\"/>`;\n    out += `<polygon points=\"${W / 2 - ha},${a.y} ${W / 2 + ha},${a.y} ${W / 2 + hb},${b.y} ${W / 2 - hb},${b.y}\" fill=\"${band ? T.road[0] : T.road[1]}\"/>`;\n  }\n  // Lane lines and glowing edges.\n  const far = proj(40), near = proj(1);\n  for (const f of [-1, -1 / 3, 1 / 3, 1]) {\n    const edge = Math.abs(f) === 1;\n    out += `<line x1=\"${W / 2 + HALF * far.s * f}\" y1=\"${far.y}\" x2=\"${W / 2 + HALF * near.s * f}\" y2=\"${near.y}\" stroke=\"${edge ? ## Claude · 14:35:13

The two directions are ready to pick from. Each shows the Start, Play and End screens side by side:

- **A — Neon Dusk:** a night city at speed. A dark road with glowing teal edges, gems that look like lit crystals, and a violet storm with lightning behind you. The motion is snappy; a 6-chain gives a white flash and a short slow-motion.
- **B — Paper Daylight:** a calmer, more Infinity feel. A warm cream sky, a terracotta road, glossy gems like boiled sweets, and an ink-blue dust wave behind you. The motion is softer and bouncier; a 6-chain throws confetti in the gem's shape instead of flashing.

Both use the same layout:
- **Top:** score on the left, best on the right, and the 6-slot chain bar in the middle with the multiplier under it.
- **Bottom:** the chaser meter.
- **Gems:** each colour has its own shape (diamond, circle, triangle, hexagon), so colour-blind players can tell them apart.

"Chroma Rush" is only a working title, so change it if you like.

**The images aren't in Figma yet.** My sandbox can't reach Figma's upload server. I made an empty Figma file called "Infinity — design directions" to hold them. The images are also saved in your folder under `design/directions/`. If you want to edit by hand, drag both PNGs into that Figma file. Otherwise just tell me what to change.

❓ **Pick A or B, plus any changes.**

➡️ A. Neon reads better at speed and makes for a striking video. It's also the clearer move into arcade, which is the point you want this prototype to make.

Once you pick, I'll lock the design and build the Play screen first: a real, playable, fake-3D road you can run on.

Trigger whenever the user wants to perform a write action or a unique read action that requires JavaScript execution in the Figma file context — e.g. create/edit/delete nodes, set up variables or tokens, build components and variants, modify auto-layout or fills, bind variables to properties, or inspect file structure programmatically.\"\ndisable-model-invocation: false\n---\n# use_figma — Figma Plugin API Skill\nUse the `use_figma` tool to execute JavaScript in Figma files via the Plugin API. All detailed reference docs live in `references/`.\n\nWhen available, call `create_session` with `fileKey` and `description` before file reads or edits (create the file first if needed), then pass the returned `sessionId` unchanged to every call that accepts it, including follow-ups and work across files.\n\n**Always include `figma-use` in the comma-separated `skillNames` parameter when calling `use_figma`. If this skill was loaded via an MCP resource, you MUST prefix the name with `resource:` (e.g. `resource:figma-use`).** This is a logging parameter used to track skill usage — it does not affect execution.\n\n**If Figma MCP tools appear as deferred tools, batch-load all their schemas in a single `ToolSearch` call** using the `select:` syntax — e.g. `ToolSearch query=\"select:use_figma,get_screenshot,get_metadata,create_new_file\"`. One round trip beats six.\n\n**MANDATORY for visible design changes:** Before the first mutating `use_figma` call, load [figma-generate-design](../figma-generate-design/SKILL.md) alongside this skill and include both names in every mutating call’s `skillNames`. This applies from a single visible property or element to a full page, screen, or composed multi-section view—even when the user calls that view a component. `figma-use` provides the Plugin API rules; `figma-generate-design` provides design-system discovery through `search_design_system`, asset importing, and incremental assembly.\n\n**Reusable library exception:** If the primary deliverable is a reusable component, component set, variant system, variable/token collection, style foundation, or other library asset intended for reuse or publication, load [figma-generate-library](../figma-generate-library/SKILL.md) instead. It provides the component-creation workflow—variable foundations, variant sets, and design-token bindings—that `figma-use` alone does not cover. For tasks that include both library assets and a composed design that uses them, load both companion skills. Read-only `use_figma` calls need neither.\n\n**3D transforms:** For perspective, depth, X/Y tilt, isometric cubes, or other static 3D poses, load [figma-3d-transforms](../figma-3d-transforms/SKILL.md) when available. It owns the gated `transform3d`, `scene3d`, and `transformOrigin` API guidance, including fields that may be absent from the general typings. Follow this skill's node-creation rules, then apply the 3D poses to the resulting nodes. If the 3D skill or APIs are unavailable, explain that 3D authoring is not enabled in this session. Flat in-plane rotation uses `node.rotation` and does not need the 3D skill.\n\nFor other APIs, load [plugin-api-standalone.index.md](references/plugin-api-standalone.index.md) to understand what is possible. When you are asked to write plugin API code, use this context to grep [plugin-api-standalone.d.ts](references/plugin-api-standalone.d.ts) for relevant types, methods, and properties. This is the definitive source of truth for the general API surface. It is a large typings file, so do not load it all at once, grep for relevant sections as needed.\n\nIMPORTANT: Whenever you work with design systems, start with [working-with-design-systems/wwds.md](references/working-with-design-systems/wwds.md) to understand the key concepts, processes, and guidelines for working with design systems in Figma. Then load the more specific references for components, variables, text styles, and effect styles as needed.\n\n## 1. Critical Rules\n1.  **Use `return` to send data back.** The return value is JSON-serialized automatically (objects, arrays, strings, numbers). Do NOT call `figma.closePlugin()` or wrap code in an async IIFE — this is handled for you.\n2.  **Write plain JavaScript with top-level `await` and `return`.** Code is automatically wrapped in an async context. Do NOT wrap in `(async () => { ... })()`.\n3.  `figma.notify()` **throws \"not implemented\"** — never use it\n3a. **Return node IDs and keep workflow state outside the Figma file.** Set human-readable component purpose and usage in `node.description` only on a `COMPONENT` or `COMPONENT_SET` — never on a frame or instance.\n3b. **Narrow before accessing type-specific properties.** Check `node.type`, use a capability guard such as `\"characters\" in node`, or prefilter with `findAllWithCriteria`. `characters` requires a text-capable node; optional chaining does not protect unsupported property access.\n4.  `console.log()` is NOT returned — use `return` for output\n5.  **Size construction calls for safe retry and validate from evidence.** Do not split a working operation solely to create validation checkpoints. Batch related work when the resulting script remains safe to retry; a complete section or page may be built in one call. Split when crossing page context, when partial execution would be difficult to recover, or after an actual failure requires a targeted retry. Return affected IDs and relevant counts, names, or bounds from each write—this counts as structural validation. Run a separate structural check only when required evidence is missing or after a relevant mutation. Normally take one screenshot after composition and on

  "command": "cat > prep.py <<'EOF'\nimport re,sys,json\nfonts={'a':{'display':'Unbounded','num':'Unbounded'},'b':{'display':'Bricolage Grotesque','num':'Bricolage Grotesque'}}\nfor d in 'ab':\n  for s in ['start','play','end']:\n    t=open(f'{d}-{s}.svg').read()\n    t=re.sub(r'(\\d+\\.\\d)\\d+',r'\\1',t)\n    t=t.replace('class=\"display\"',f'font-family=\"{fonts[d][\"display\"]}\" font-weight=\"800\"' if d=='b' else f'font-family=\"{fonts[d][\"display\"]}\" font-weight=\"700\"')\n    t=t.replace('class=\"num\"',f'font-family=\"{fonts[d][\"num\"]}\" font-weight=\"700\"')\n    t=t.replace('class=\"lbl\"','font-family=\"Space Grotesk\" font-weight=\"700\"')\n    if 'xmlns=' not in t: t=t.replace('<svg ','<svg xmlns=\"http://www.w3.org/2000/svg\" ',1)\n    open(f'{d}-{s}.min.svg','w').write(t); print(d,s,len(t))\nEOF\npython3 prep.py",
  "description": "Inline fonts and shrink SVGs for Figma import"
}

{ ...p, color: fromHex(h) } : p; }\n  if (p.type && p.type.startsWith('GRADIENT')) {\n    return { ...p, gradientStops: p.gradientStops.map(s => { const h = map[toHex(s.color)]; return h ? { ...s, color: { ...fromHex(h), a: s.color.a } } : s; }) };\n  }\n  return p;\n};\nlet n = 0;\nfor (const node of [B, ...B.findAll(() => true)]) {\n  if ('fills' in node && Array.isArray(node.fills)) { node.fills = node.fills.map(remapPaint); n++; }\n  if ('strokes' in node && Array.isArray(node.strokes)) node.strokes = node.strokes.map(remapPaint);\n  if ('effects' in node && node.effects.length) node.effects = node.effects.map(e => e.type === 'LAYER_BLUR' && e.radius < 9 ? x - t.width : align === 'CENTER' ? x - t.width/2 : x; t.y = y;\n  created.push(t.id); return t;\n};\nfor (const [frame, font, ink, muted, combo] of [[A, fA, 'f6f2ea', 'a49fc4', 'ff4d5e'], [B, fB, '2b2a3a', '7b6f66', 'f0553f']]) {\n  addText(frame, '12,400', font, 30, ink, 22, 14);\n  addText(frame, 'BEST', font, 11, muted, 383, 28, 'RIGHT');\n  addText(frame, '18,900', font, 16, muted, 383, 42, 'RIGHT');\n  addText(frame, '×2', font, 22, combo, 202.5, 142, 'CENTER');\n}\n// Row titles above each frame.\naddText(figma.currentPage, 'A — Neon Dusk', fA, 24, '1a1236', 100, 50);\naddText(figma.currentPage, 'B — Paper Daylight', fB, 24, '2b2a3a', 600, 50);\nawait figma.currentPage.screenshot ? null : null;\nreturn { createdNodeIds: created, recoloured: n, fonts: [fA, fB] };"

const browser = await launch();
const page = await browser.newPage({ viewport });
page.on('console', (m) => { if (m.type() === 'error') problems.push(`console: ${m.text()}`); });
page.on('pageerror', (e) => problems.push(`page error: ${e.message}`));
page.on('requestfailed', (r) => problems.push(`request failed: ${r.url()} (${r.failure()?.errorText})`));
page.on('response', (r) => { if (r.status() >= 400) problems.push(`HTTP ${r.status()}: ${r.url()}`); });

async function shot(name) {
  if (!shots) return;
  await mkdir(shotDir, { recursive: true });
  await page.waitForTimeout(400); // let entrance animations settle
  await page.screenshot({ path: path.join(shotDir, `${name}.png`) });
}

let checked = 'page loads';
try {
  await page.goto(url, { waitUntil: 'load' });
  await page.evaluate(() => { try { localStorage.clear(); } catch { /* ignore */ } });
  await page.reload({ waitUntil: 'load' });
  await page.waitForTimeout(300);
  await shot('00-landing');

  const hooks = await page.evaluate(() => ({
    game: Boolean(window.__GAME__),
    screens: Array.isArray(window.__GAME__?.screens) ? window.__GAME__.screens : [],
    smoke: typeof window.__GAME__?.smoke === 'function',
  }));

  if (hooks.game && shots) {
    for (const [i, name] of hooks.screens.entries()) {
      await page.evaluate((n) => window.__GAME__.goTo?.(n), name);
      await shot(`${String(i + 1).padStart(2, '0')}-${name}`);
    }
    checked += `, ${hooks.screens.length} screens captured`;
  }
  if (hooks.smoke) {
    await page.reload({ waitUntil: 'load' });
    const gameProblems = await page.evaluate(() => window.__GAME__.smoke());
    problems.push(...(gameProblems ?? []).map((p) => `game: ${p}`));
    checked += ', game smoke flow';
  }
} catch (e) {
  problems.push(`aborted: ${e.message}`);
} finally {
  await browser.close();
  server?.close();
}

if (problems.length) {
  console.error(`SMOKE FAILED (${problems.length}) at ${url}\n  ${problems.join('\n  ')}`);
  process.exit(1);
}
console.log(`Smoke passed at ${url} (${viewport.width}×${viewport.height}): ${checked}; no console or network errors.${shots ? ' Screenshots: design/compare/' : ''}`);

## Done-when check
`npm test` passes with 22/22 tests green (14 engine tests + 8 new storage tests). All required test cases are verified:
1. `defaults when nothing is saved`: returns `{ version: 1, best: 0, runs: 0, settings: { sound: true } }`.
2. `save then load round-trips best and settings.sound`: verifies round-trip and persistence under `STORAGE_KEY`.
3. `update(fn) applies fn`: correctly transforms and saves data via callback.
4. `reset() returns defaults`: resets both localStorage and module memory to defaults.
5. `corrupt JSON in storage returns defaults without throwing`: parses bad JSON gracefully.
6. `an unknown version keeps best but resets the rest`: preserves `best` while wiping outdated fields and restoring default version/settings.
7. `works with no localStorage at all (memory fallback)`: verifies functional memory fallback for `save`, `load`, `update`, and `reset`.
8. `a localStorage whose methods throw does not throw`: verifies error suppression across throwing methods (`getItem`, `setItem`, `removeItem`) and throwing property getter.
## Notes for Claude
- `STORAGE_KEY` and `VERSION` were imported dynamically from `public/src/storage.js` without hard-coding.
- `globalThis.localStorage` is faked per test and cleaned up in `beforeEach` and `afterEach` along with module `reset()`.
- No bugs encountered in `storage.js`.

# Audio Events and ElevenLabs Prompts — Chain Runner

Audio event specifications for the colour-chain runner. Sounds conform to the Infinity Games style guide: bright, musical, tuned glass, soft synth, quiet acoustics, confirming progress without jarring or alarming the player.

## Sound Event Matrix

| Event | When it fires | Feel | Duration (s) | ElevenLabs Prompt |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `lane` | Player shifts left or right (`step` action) | Smooth, swift aerodynamic air whoosh with subtle glassy chime | 0.25 | Soft airy whoosh of speed with delicate glass resonance, smooth aerodynamic swipe, gentle neon glide, quiet high-end chime, clean transient. |
| `edge` | Player attempts to shift beyond the outer lane boundary | Muted, soft rubbery friction or cushioned bumper tap, no harsh buzz | 0.20 | Gentle cushioned thud with a soft rubber rebound, subtle low-frequency dampening, smooth tactile bumper bump, non-punitive and muffled. |
| `jump` | Player initiates a jump over the road (`step` jump) | Weightless upward lift, airy synth chime ascending | 0.35 | Soft upward synth sweep with an airy glass flutter, light aerodynamic launch, weightless ascending tone, delicate melodic swoosh. |
